# BioAgent Prototype Handoff

## 2026-09-30 (midnight) — the tool-folder refactor is on main; the public mirror carries everything but credentials

`claude/aiscientist-architecture-refactor-b0fcaa` is merged into main with a merge commit (one
`git revert -m 1` undoes it). Not deployed: the next `scripts/sync_deploy.sh` ships it. After that
deploy, start one scRNA research run and ask the chat one `deep_literature` question, to see the
HPC3 jobs start from the new paths.

- **Merging main into the branch:** main's deep_literature follow-up (`7933492`) landed in
  `deep_literature/tool.py` through rename detection. `split_tools.py --regenerate-from main` now
  reproduces `_lib/scrna.py` byte for byte; a docstring edit made by hand during the split had been

## 2026-09-30 (night) — the repository is ready to split into three; one folder per tool

Branch `claude/aiscientist-architecture-refactor-b0fcaa` (not merged, not deployed). Plan, procedures
and evidence: `docs/architecture/REPO_SPLIT.md`; architecture overview with diagrams:

https://www.figma.com/board/OHeckC4jS6Yw5ET9UjBeQB (AiScientist team); bilingual handbook artifact
https://claude.ai/artifact/DGS3xvnUXERqth18EPH7if.

**Target:** RCHENLAB/AiScientist (platform), RCHENLAB/AiScientist-tools (the model-callable tools),
RCHENLAB/AiScientist-skills (skills + pipelines). Import path stays `bioagent.tools` (namespace
package), so no `python -m bioagent.tools...` entry point or CLAUDE.md namespace rule changes.

**What changed on the branch (no behaviour change):**
- `tools/sdk.py` is the tool contract (`HarnessTool` moved there; `session_chat_fn` replaces the two

**Found while doing it:**

- After the split, an old editable install that still ships `bioagent/__init__.py` silently hides

**Evidence:** full suite 2019 passed / 3 skipped on the branch. Split rehearsal on fresh clones: the
tools repository alone 346 passed; the platform with the tools on the path and skills from their own
checkout 1670 passed / 3 skipped. Merge rehearsal (real new commits on main + simulated fixes in

**Merging this week while the tools are being fixed on main:** `git merge main`, keep the split

## 2026-09-30 (evening) — a real deploy no longer logs its rsync as "(dry-run)"

`917cd79` (`scripts/sync_deploy.sh`), committed on main; not pushed. Nothing to deploy: the script
runs on the operator's machine. Closes the "(dry-run)" item under "Found on the way" in the
gene-set section below.

- **Symptom:** the ddf043c deploy printed "rsync (dry-run) -> staging
  eyeserver:/tmp/bioagent-deploy-yijunsun/" although it shipped the code and restarted the service
  (md5 on `/data/BioAgent/app` matched). An operator reading the log could conclude nothing was
  deployed.
- **Cause:** both rsync log lines used `${DRY_RUN:+(dry-run) }`, which expands for any non-empty
  value, and `DRY_RUN` starts at 0, not empty.
- **Fix:** the label is computed once from the numeric flag (`_dry`) and used on the direct and the
  sudo path.
- **Verified:** `bash -n`; the script with ssh/rsync stubbed and an unresolvable host, both paths ×
  real/`--dry-run`: before the fix all four printed "(dry-run)", after it only the two dry-runs. A
  real `--dry-run` against prod still prints it and stops before the privileged step.

## 2026-09-30 (afternoon) — coverage counts come from the tools; a fork the plan answered is not asked

Deployed as `33d9997` at 12:46 PDT.

**Coverage counts.** Run f3731e0b7136's manuscript said Endothelial "7 DDX41 / 27 WT", HC "5 / 11"
and a Microglia WT arm of 24 fell below the 30-cell floor. Those are the uploaded file's counts;
run_de tested and refused 5 / 22, 4 / 9 and 57 / 21 after QC, which removed 30% of nuclei.
- **Cause:** the claim-audit block gave both writers the dataset profile's per-arm counts as the
  only authority ("if a step states different counts, those counts are wrong: use these"). A step's
  run_code answer and the plan had also quoted the file's counts.
- **Fix, prevention:** the block now has two tables. The uploaded file (before QC) is only for
  describing the data as uploaded; the counts the tools tested (`cells_by_group_and_arm`,
  `skipped_groups`, passed as `audit(..., tested_counts=)`) must be used for every statement about
  the analysis. The grounding labels the refused groups' counts "cells after QC", and the PI prompt
  labels the profile table "in the uploaded file, before QC".
- **Fix, guarantee:** `correct_coverage_counts` replaces, in a coverage sentence only, a refused
  group's count that equals its pre-QC count with the tested one; a sentence about the file before
  QC is left alone. It runs in `verify_report_facts` and on the gateway manuscript
  (`_correct_manuscript_coverage` in `_build_report`), whose corrections land in
  `result.claim_audit["report_corrections"]`. Replayed on the real report: one line changed, the
  five wrong pairs.

**The labels-vs-re-cluster prompt.** It was asked in both runs, and both times for nothing.
- **f107bcf7b660:** "reuse them and do not re-cluster" matched the clustering regex, because `\b`
  matches after the hyphen.
- **f3731e0b7136:** the DAG structurer copied the fork from its prompt's example onto "validate the
  existing majorclass labels", in a plan with no clustering step.
- **Fix:** a negated mention no longer counts as clustering (`_clusters_de_novo`). A label fork is
  cleared, with a `decision_settled` event, when no step clusters de-novo. The structurer prompt
  says not to flag a fork the step text already decides. Replayed on both real plans: neither
  would ask.

**Why deep_literature kept timing out** (the fix is the separate task running on HPC3):
- PaperQA's LLM calls go through litellm with a 60 s timeout and send no reasoning effort, so
  Qwen3.8 thinks at its default xhigh. At ~70 tok/s one summary call took ~3 minutes (log:
  "time taken=181.42 seconds").
- vLLM keeps generating a request after the client gives up (/metrics: abort 0), so abandoned
  calls hold the GPU while the retries queue behind them.
- Qwen3.6 was fast enough to fit in 60 s; the 2026-09-25 switch to Qwen3.8 broke it silently.
- Cross-ref: measured on HPC3 afterwards, see `handoff/ziyao/HANDOFF.md` 2026-09-30. The 181 s was
  the answer call (3 × 60 s), not a summary. vLLM does stop an abandoned request; "abort 0" only
  means vLLM 0.28 does not count client disconnects.

Tests: full suite 1976 passed, 3 skipped.

## 2026-09-30 (latest) — Qwen3.8's first real execution run; the Scientist had no reasoning effort

**Deployed:** main `a9ea9ab` at 03:19 PDT, then `6e7c95a` at 07:17 PDT. Together they add the
doublet message fix, the gene-set deploy guard, per-run dependency installs, and the three fixes
below. The serve job was sized 3 CPU / 18G earlier the same night.

**Run f107bcf7b660** (DDX41 vs WT, plan mode, Qwen3.8-27B-INT4 on an RTX6000) was the first real
execution run on Qwen3.8.
- **Planning:** 38 min to the plan card: team research, then one ~35k-token PI call at xhigh. The
  plan itself was good: 13 steps, with the 1.6x depth gap treated as a confound from step 1.
- **First steps:** step 1 (provenance) was accepted at 0.95 and step 2 (QC) at 0.82. QC reported
  `read_from` and `mito_rule`, and "QC removed nothing" (the data were pre-filtered).
- **Step 3 stalled for about 20 minutes.** Every lab role sends a per-role `reasoning_effort`, but
  the Scientist's tool turns (`vllm_client.chat_tools`) sent none, so Qwen3.8 thought at its default,
  xhigh. Turns ended at the 8192-token `BIOAGENT_SCIENTIST_MAX_TOKENS` with finish_reason "length"
  and no tool call; vLLM /metrics counted 15 of 444 requests cut. The Critic scored the step 0.05,
  and after 3 attempts it raised a failure fork.
- **Fix `f4b0e56`:** `chat_tools` sends an effort (`BIOAGENT_VLLM_REASONING_EFFORT_SCIENTIST`, else
  the global `BIOAGENT_VLLM_REASONING_EFFORT`, medium in prod). It retries without the field if the
  model rejects the level, and returns finish_reason. `scientist_chat` asks once more at low effort
  when a turn ends at the ceiling with no tool call.
- **Fix `a9ea9ab`:** Stop x2 ended the step, but the failure fork raised AFTER Stop held the stopped
  run open for the full 600 s decision timeout. `_decision_review` (now module-level) returns
  cancel at once when the run's stop flag is set.
- **Driver:** `scripts/e2e_prod_drive.py` now has `--plan-timeout` (default 1 h) and
  `--run-timeout`, and it answers mid-run decision prompts with the first option. The run had also
  paused on "use the existing labels or re-cluster?", a fork the approved plan had already settled.

**Per-run dependency install, verified through the deployed executor** (a script on the eyeserver
using the production `SlurmAnalysisExecutor`, `.env`, and the a9ea9ab source on HPC3):
- QC ok, then doublet detection reported scikit-image missing;
- the executor installed `scikit-image==0.25.2` for the run, and the retry was ok (0.25%);
- a second call reused the install;
- `release_run_deps()` removed `_deps`;
- the console lines read "…installing it on HPC3 for this run only" and "Installed … running … again".

**Rerun f3731e0b7136** on a9ea9ab (same question, same data):
- **Timing:** ready in 167 s on an RTX6000. Plan card after 52 min (14 steps); 20 of those minutes
  were one PaperQA job whose LLM calls kept timing out. Execution ran 04:14 to 06:45, the write-up
  06:45 to 07:16. The serve job hit its 2 h limit at 05:20 and was re-provisioned on another
  RTX6000 within seconds; vLLM was back about 3 minutes later.
- **Execution:** 14/14 steps accepted, converged. 159 Scientist tool calls, 13% not ok: 19 run_code
  bugs, such as guessed column names, `class=` used as a keyword argument, and a full dense SVD of a
  15k × 22k matrix. **No Scientist turn was cut at the ceiling** (vLLM "length": 0 during execution,
  against 15 before the fix).
- **The new parameters in use:**
  - every curated result carried `read_from`;
  - the model used `input` once, pointing run_depth_matched_de at adata_qc.h5ad, because no
    clustering step had made adata_clustered.h5ad;
  - the Critic's review of the stratified run_de step cites the tool's skipped groups and warnings:
    "all match the tool's structured outputs exactly … correctly derived from the tool's own
    warnings".
- **HITL:** the driver answered two decision prompts. One failure fork (cross-cell-type synthesis
  failed 3×) recovered through the "probe the upstream tables first" option and was accepted at
  0.87.
- **Report:** 5,539 words plus the technical report, after 3 visual-review passes. It is explicit
  about the 1.61x depth gap, the missing replicates, rankings being descriptive, the depth-matched
  checks, ambient RNA for the phototransduction genes in bipolar cells, and light/circadian state
  for the glycolysis signals. One numeric slip: "Groups not covered" quotes PRE-QC counts from the
  raw profile (Endothelial 7/27 etc.), while the 30-cell floor was applied to post-QC counts
  (run_de `skipped_groups`: 5/22, HC 4/9, Microglia 57/21).
- **Found and fixed (`6e7c95a`, deployed):** after the 05:20 swap, every deep_literature job still
  called the dead endpoint, because its URL was captured at executor build. `live_args` now re-reads
  the current allocation at every job.

**Open:**
- **deep_literature (literature line; a task was filed):** it returned 0 sources in every call that
  night. PaperQA's litellm calls to Qwen3.8 time out at 60 s (16 timeouts in one job) and send no
  reasoning effort. Fixed in `ddf043c`, deployed 16:15 PDT; see
  `handoff/ziyao/HANDOFF.md`, 2026-09-30.
- **Critic view of run_code output:** the digest's 1,000-character string cap cuts run_code stdout,
  and the Critic noted two numbers that "fall in the truncated portion". Keep the tail, or raise the
  cap for stdout.
- **Uncovered-group counts:** they should come from the tool's `skipped_groups` (post-QC), not from
  the dataset profile.
- **Redundant decision prompt:** "use the existing labels or re-cluster?" is asked even when the
  approved plan already says which.

## 2026-09-30 (later) — a tool's declared dependency is installed for the run; the RTX6000 serve job fits again

**Dependencies installed for one run (Yijun: install temporarily, clean up when the run ends).**
The section below found that `run_doublet_detection` could never run on HPC3, because analysis.sif
has no scikit-image.
- **What happens:** when a curated tool reports `dependency_missing` for a dependency listed in
  `tools/run_deps.ALLOWED`, `SlurmAnalysisExecutor` does three things:
  - submits an install job (`scrna_cli --tool _install_dependency`, same image, same node type);
  - puts the package in the run's own HPC3 workspace, `<run>/_deps`;
  - runs the tool again, once, with the result carrying `dependency_installed`.
  Later steps in the run reuse the install. At publish, `release_run_deps()` deletes `<run>/_deps`;
  the 3-day Temp sweep is the backstop.
- **What may be installed:** only names in `ALLOWED`, where the version is pinned in code. Today
  that is `scikit-image==0.25.2`, reachable under both spellings a tool can report. A model-chosen
  package never takes this path; run_code keeps its one-confirmation preflight, because a
  hallucinated name can be a typosquatted package. `_install_dependency` is not a model tool.
- **The image stays in charge:**
  - wheels only (`--only-binary=:all:`), so no install-time scripts run;
  - every top-level module the image already provides is removed from the install, together with
    its vendored `.libs`;
  - the directory is APPENDED to `sys.path` by the generated sitecustomize;
  - the package must import from the install in a fresh interpreter, or the install is removed and
    reported as failed.
- **Failures are recorded, not retried:** a failed install keeps the tool's own
  `dependency_missing`, adds `dependency_install: failed` with the reason, and is not attempted
  again in the run. `BIOAGENT_AUTO_INSTALL_DEPS=0` turns the feature off.
- **Verified on HPC3** (analysis.sif, the real Ddx41 data):
  - doublet detection first reports `dependency_missing: scikit-image`;
  - the install prunes PIL, networkx, numpy, packaging and scipy, leaving 42.6 MB (116 MB before
    the `.libs` pruning);
  - the retried tool returns ok, with a doublet rate of 0.25% (the data were DoubletFinder-filtered
    upstream);
  - numpy 2.4.6 and scipy 1.17.1 still import from the image's site-packages, and only skimage
    comes from `<run>/_deps`.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_run_deps.py` (7; the import check runs a real interpreter) and 7 executor
  cases in `test_slurm_analysis.py`.

**The Qwen serve job: 4 CPU / 24G → 3 CPU / 18G (prod `.env`).** Yijun: use the RTX6000 partition,
one card is enough, and prefer it by default. The candidate order already did that; the job just
could not land there.
- **Why it could not land:** the idle RTX6000 cards were on nodes with 2-3 free CPUs (m54-01 and
  n54-03 had none). free-gpu32 has `MaxMemPerCPU=6144`, which turns 24G into 4 CPUs.
- **Probes:** jobs of 3 CPU / 18G and 2 CPU / 12G both started in about 30 s. 2 CPU / 24G still
  pends, because it is bumped back to 4 CPUs.
- **Measured need:** vLLM serve-job peak RSS was 12.6G, 11.7G and 10.6G (sacct MaxRSS), so 18G has
  headroom and 12G does not.
- **Result:** the next connect got an RTX6000 in 20 s, and vLLM was ready in 183 s.
- **Backup and docs:** the backup is `/data/BioAgent/env-backups/.env.bak-20260930-slurmmem`. It
  moved out of the app dir because `sync_deploy --delete` would delete `.env.bak*` there.
  `configs/aiscientist.example.env` now explains the sizing.

## 2026-09-30 — a `--delete` deploy can no longer remove the gene-set libraries

**The exposure.** The offline enrichment libraries
`src/bioagent/tools/genesets/{GO_Biological_Process_2023,Reactome_2022,MSigDB_Hallmark_2020}.gmt`
are gitignored (`scripts/fetch_genesets.py` downloads them), so a fresh worktree has none.
`scripts/sync_deploy.sh --delete` from such a tree would delete them from `/data/BioAgent/app`, and
`--delete` is the mode the script's own help recommends for a divergent server. The gateway's
`_sync_bioagent_source_to_hpc` then `rm -rf`s HPC3 `pysrc/<user>/bioagent` and re-tars the live
package, so the next session would lose them on HPC3 too. `run_enrichment` and `run_gsea_prerank`
refuse to run without them.

It came close. The 2026-09-30 deploy of 8f1cec9 ran from the worktree branch
`claude/vigorous-tereshkova-768267`, which had no `.gmt`. `.deployed_sha` names that branch, and the
server's staging dir still holds that tree, with only `README.md` in `genesets/`. Prod kept its
copies only because that run did not pass `--delete`. This closes the "Gene sets and deploys"
follow-up in the deploy section below.

**Measured before the fix** (read-only dry-runs against the real prod tree):
- the privileged staging → app rsync with `--delete`, on the server's rsync 3.4.1: `*deleting` for
  all three;
- the direct path (`SUDO=""`) from this worktree: the same three, among 134 `*deleting` lines.

**Fix (`scripts/sync_deploy.sh`).**
- `PROTECT_PATTERNS=('/src/bioagent/tools/genesets/*.gmt')`, passed as `--filter 'P …'`. A protect
  rule only stops the receiver's deletions, so the files still ship when the local tree has them.
  An exclude would have stopped that too.
- The rule is on the direct path and on the privileged staging → app rsync. It is NOT on the
  staging mirror, on purpose: staging must mirror this tree exactly. Otherwise a `.gmt` left in
  staging by an earlier deploy would ride into the app dir and overwrite the server's copy.
- The privileged step takes the rule as a `--filter` argument, not as a line in the
  `--exclude-from` file. rsync reads that file as exclude patterns, parsing only `- ` and `+ `
  prefixes, so a `P …` line there is a literal pattern that matches nothing. On the server's rsync
  3.4.1, against the real app dir, that form still deleted all three; the Mac's openrsync does the
  same on scratch dirs.
- The privileged rsync command is built once (`PRIV_RSYNC`). `--dry-run` now prints the exact
  command the real run executes, protect rule included, where it used to print a summary
  (`rsync --delete staging -> app`).
- A tree with no `.gmt` gets a warning: the app dir keeps its copies, and this deploy does not
  update them. On the sudo path it adds that a `*deleting …genesets/*.gmt` line from the STAGING
  rsync is expected.

**Verified.** The dry-runs against prod are read-only; the rest ran on scratch dirs.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `sync_deploy.sh --dry-run --delete` (sudo path) from this worktree | warning shown; the staging rsync has no `.gmt` lines; the plan prints `--filter='P /src/bioagent/tools/genesets/*.gmt'` |
| that printed privileged command, run on the server through the same `bash -lc "…"` wrapper with `--dry-run -i` added | no `.gmt` deletions; the same command without the `P` rule deletes all three |
| `SUDO="" … --dry-run --delete` (direct path, openrsync → rsync 3.4.1) | 131 `*deleting` lines instead of 134; the only difference is the three `.gmt` |
| the tree HAS the `.gmt` (copied in, then removed) | the direct path sends all three; staging receives all three; no warning |
| the privileged command on scratch dirs | staging lacks them → the app keeps the old file; staging has a new one → the app gets it; a stray file is still deleted |

**Status:** committed on `claude/keen-allen-04bed1`; not merged, not pushed, not deployed. The
protection lives in the script the operator runs, not on the server: it applies to any deploy run
from a checkout that has this commit. Nothing on prod has to change.

**Found on the way, not changed:**
- `scripts/deploy_interactive.sh` defaults to `--delete` and has no protect rule, so running it
  from a worktree would delete the `.gmt`. It also lacks the `ssh_creds/` and `llm_creds/` excludes,
  and it restarts through `start.sh`. `sync_deploy.sh` is the supported path.
- The same `--delete` dry-run would remove 108 other prod files (131 `*deleting` lines with their
  directories). Most are things the repo has since moved or dropped: the pre-rename `skills/`
  copies (471aa3c), old `docs/` and `tests/` files, `.playwright-mcp/`, caches, and a 66 MB
  `Ddx41_DEG.h5ad`. It would also remove the three `.env.bak*` rollback copies of the prod `.env`,
  and `.deploy.env`. Review that list before any `--delete` run.
- `${DRY_RUN:+(dry-run) }` expands when `DRY_RUN=0` too, so a real deploy's rsync line also says
  "(dry-run)". Cosmetic. **Fixed in `917cd79`** (2026-09-30 evening, above).

## 2026-09-30 — 8f1cec9 deployed and tested on HPC3; doublet detection could not run there

**Deploy.** main `8f1cec9` (the three sections below: `input` and mito, the raised limits, the
kept caveats) went live at 01:03:50 PDT through `scripts/sync_deploy.sh`, without `--delete`.
- **Prod was idle first:** no run file had changed in 3 days, and HPC3 had no AiScientist jobs (a
  restart kills live runs).
- **No drift:** every file of `2cdb91b` under src/, frontend/, preset_pipelines/, skills/ and
  configs/ matched prod byte for byte before the deploy. The 43 prod-only leftovers are untouched.
- **After:** the changed files' md5 match, the service is active, and HTTP returns 200.
- **Correction:** `6f30332` (step-count check) and `da52541` (scGPT mouse) were already live; they
  are part of `2cdb91b`, deployed 2026-09-28.

**End-to-end run: not done.** `scripts/e2e_prod_drive.py` connected, but `ensure_serve_job` waits
300 s for a GPU by design, and none came:
- the free RTX6000 queue had 42 jobs ahead of ours;
- all 8 A100s were busy, with an estimated start of 10:52.
The connect failed cleanly and cancelled both candidates. Check `squeue --start` before driving one.

**Tool test in the production container instead.** A test script called the tools through
`scrna_cli.run_tool`, as the Slurm job does, with plain anndata code standing in for run_code steps.
It ran as a Slurm job on a `free` CPU node, in `analysis.sif` (scanpy 1.11.5, anndata 0.12.19,
pandas 2.3.3, numpy 2.4.6), on the deployed source (checked by md5) and the real
`uploads/yijus12/Ddx41_DEG.h5ad` (15,307 cells). **30/31 passed:**
- **Default chain:** QC reads the bound dataset. The default rule matches the 13 mouse `mt-` genes,
  and the result carries `read_from` and `mito_rule`.
- **`input` from run_code files:**
  - a subset saved under a new name → run_clustering(input) → run_de continues the standard chain
    (`read_from` = work/adata_clustered.h5ad);
  - an MG-only subset through `$BIOAGENT_WORK/…` → run_de (787 DDX41 / 535 WT);
  - GSEA on a ranking written by run_code (upper-cased symbols, 9,720 genes), with the statistic
    reported "as supplied".
- **Ensembl-keyed copy** (symbols moved to `var['feature_name']`):
  - the default rule warns and names `feature_name`;
  - at `max_pct_mt=3`, `gene_symbols_key` removes exactly the cells the symbol-keyed data loses
    (15,307 → 13,740);
  - without the key, nothing is removed and the result says so.
- **Refusals:** a path outside the run, a symlink out of it, a missing file (the error lists work/)
  and an unknown var column are all refused.

**Caveats in the views, on real output.** This run_de result has 39 keys. Its reporting keys sit at
positions 27, 34, 36, 37 and 38, and the last four are past the old 30-key cut. All of them now
reach the digest in full; none of the 4 warnings is shortened.

**Found: run_doublet_detection could never run on HPC3.**
- **Cause:** `analysis.sif` has no scikit-image. scanpy 1.11.5 then says "requires skimage, but
  skimage is not installed", while the tool matched only "scikit-image" (scanpy 1.12's wording).
  So every call returned an opaque `error` instead of `dependency_missing`. No archived run had
  called it; the Ddx41 data arrive already DoubletFinder-filtered.
- **Fixed:** both wordings are recognised, and the test pins both. It fails on the old code for the
  1.11.5 wording. Full suite: 1930 passed, 3 skipped.
- **Not deployed.** The package itself is still missing.

**Two gotchas found while testing:**
- **Transfer host:** `access-hpc3.rcic.uci.edu` takes only scp/rsync (a restricted shell). Use it
  to stage files, not the login node. Compute nodes need `module load singularity/3.11.3` before
  `singularity`.
- **Gene sets and deploys:** the gene sets (`tools/genesets/*.gmt`) are gitignored, so a fresh
  worktree has none. A `sync_deploy.sh --delete` from a worktree would delete them from prod, and
  the next HPC3 sync would delete them there. A task to protect them was filed.

**Open, waiting on Yijun:**
- auto-installing missing dependencies temporarily, with cleanup at run end, versus the lab-shared
  cache;
- the end-to-end run once a GPU is free;
- scGPT on mouse: there is no official mouse checkpoint; the proposal is a curated model registry
  plus a mouse-native reference.

## 2026-09-29 — analysis tools can read a file run_code wrote; the mitochondrial rule is a parameter

The analysis tools found their input by a FIXED name: `adata_qc.h5ad` → `adata_clustered.h5ad` →
`adata_de.h5ad`, and run_de's `rank_<groupby>_<group>.rnk` for GSEA. None took an input path. So
an upstream step done in run_code broke the chain. In the 9 archived runs:
- 3 of the 8 failed curated-tool calls were "checkpoint missing";
- 3 run_code snippets overwrote a tool's checkpoint to feed the next tool, which desynchronises the
  tables already exported from it.
The only other way round was to redo the rest of the line in run_code. run_code fails 43% of the
time and carries none of the tools' guards.

**`input`** — optional, default `""`, meaning the old behaviour:
- **Which tools:** run_scanpy_qc, run_clustering, run_de, run_depth_matched_de,
  run_doublet_detection, run_integration, run_pseudobulk_de, run_composition and
  run_marker_annotation take an `.h5ad`. run_gsea_prerank takes a `.rnk` file or a file-name
  pattern; each file is one group, and the ranking statistic is reported as "as supplied".
- **Where it may point** (`scrna_pack._run_files`): only inside this run's `work/` or `artifacts/`.
  A bare name, `work/…`, `$BIOAGENT_WORK/…` and an absolute path inside the run are all accepted.
  Anything outside is refused, including through a symlink or `..`. A missing file is refused with
  the list of what IS in `work/`.
- **What the result records:** every result gains `read_from`, the file actually read. A set
  `input` is recorded by `nondefault_params` (kind `selection`), so the technical report says which
  step read a file of the model's choosing.
- **Where results are written:** outputs keep their standard names, so the chain carries on from
  the tool. run_doublet_detection, which rewrites in place, rewrites the `input` file and never
  adata_qc.h5ad.
- **Brief:** it now says to write a variant under a NEW name and pass it as `input`, and not to
  re-implement a tool in run_code to use data prepared in code.

**Mitochondrial rule.** QC matched mito genes only by the name prefix `MT-`/`mt-`. Data keyed by
Ensembl IDs, as cellxgene ships it, got no mito filter at all. There are two new `PARAMS`, which
are also documented in the DE protocol table as `test_declared_params` requires:
- `mito_prefix`, default `"MT-"`, case-insensitive. For example `mt:` for Drosophila.
- `gene_symbols_key`, a `var` column holding the symbols, for example `feature_name`.
When nothing matches, the warning now names the var column that WOULD match and the parameter to
set. The result carries `mito_rule`.

**Not made a parameter, on purpose:** run_de's direction-bias rule (≥100 significant genes and a
5× skew) and the extreme-fold-change cut (|log2FC| ≥ 5). They change nothing that is computed;
they decide which WARNINGS fire. A model allowed to set them could switch off its own caveats.

**Tests:**
- `tests/test_tool_input.py`: 12 tests. They cover the path rules, the untouched default chain, the
  "QC done in run_code" dead end end to end on real scanpy, QC from a file, and GSEA on the model's
  own lists.
- `test_scrna_advanced.py`: +4 (doublets write back to the input file; composition reads the input;
  every advanced tool refuses a path outside the run).
- `test_tool_self_diagnosis.py`: +4. On Ensembl-keyed data, `gene_symbols_key` removes exactly the
  100 mitochondria-rich cells that were kept before; other cases cover the Drosophila prefix and an
  unknown column.
- Full suite: 1928 passed, 3 skipped.

`docs/ENVIRONMENT.md` was regenerated. The tools' `file:line` entries had moved, and it still
showed the reference path that 2cdb91b fixed.

**Status:** not deployed. The tools run on HPC3 from the source the gateway syncs at session start,
so a normal deploy plus a new session picks this up; no image rebuild is needed.

## 2026-09-28 (latest) — the tool-result size limits are raised

The section below keeps caveats inside the limits. This one raises the limits themselves.
The old ones dated from a much smaller window: the harness budgets against 262,144 tokens, and
run_code / run_shell / read_tool_source already cap their own output at 20,000 characters, yet the
Scientist was fed at most 4,000 of it.

| limit (`agents/research_harness.py` unless noted) | was | now |
|---|---|---|
| `_FEED_RESULT_CHARS`: one tool result fed to the Scientist | 4,000 | 24,000 |
| `_DIGEST_MAX_STR`: each string in the Critic / writer digest | 300 | 1,000 |
| `_DIGEST_MAX_ITEMS`: each list in the digest | 10 | 20 |
| `_DIGEST_MAX_KEYS`: non-reporting keys per dict in the digest | 30 | 50 |
| `_DIGEST_ANSWER_CHARS`: each tool in the auto-summary answer | 400 | 1,200 |
| `quick_chat.QuickChatConfig.max_result_chars` | 4,000 | 8,000 |

Measured on the 561 archived tool results (9 runs), against the pre-fix views:
- **Feed:** results cut 98 → 0; reporting values cut 19 → 0; characters fed +37.6%.
- **Digest:** +26.7% in total. The Critic's tool results per step grow +24% (median 8.2K → 10.2K
  characters, max 35K → 39K). The writer's artifacts per run grow +22% (median 33K → 40K, max
  95K → 127K). Warnings cut mid-sentence: 28 → 0.
- **c135's run_de** now reaches both views whole, including the `significant_by_group` that the
  4,000 feed had pushed out.

**Unchanged on purpose:**
- Compressed history only applies when the window is actually full, and it is what makes the room.
  That covers the `_COMPRESS_*` heads and the stub an old tool result collapses to, which now has
  its own sizes (`_COMPRESS_DIGEST_SIZES`, the old 300/10/30). The first version reused the bigger
  digest there, and `test_exact_counter_tightens_until_real_count_fits` caught it.
- The UI previews (`[:120]` / `[:200]` on emitted events) never reach a model.
- The step-answer excerpts in the meeting prompts (`_plan_state` 300, post-step 400, explore 1,200)
  are on flag-gated paths. The main brief (`_accepted_findings_block`) already carries full answers.

**Cost:** more prompt tokens, which is free on our GPUs but paid for BYO-key users. Most of the
extra is the Scientist re-reading big results on later turns of the same step; vLLM's prefix cache
absorbs that locally. Tests read the limits from the module, so re-tuning one is a one-line change.

## 2026-09-28 (later) — large tool results keep their caveats in the Critic's and Scientist's views

This closes the "Open, not fixed here" item in "a step's answer is checked against its own tool
results" below. Both shortened views of a tool result cut by POSITION, and tools put their caveats
last:

- **Critic view: `result_digest`** kept the first 30 keys of a dict. The same digest also feeds the
  report writer's per-step artifacts (`_synthesize`), `_plan_state` and the post-step review,
  `_explore_after_step` and compressed history. run_de returns 36–38 keys, with `skipped_groups`
  and `warnings` at 33–36, so the digest dropped both in 8847d521ba32 and c135ae589d96. `warnings`
  still reached the Critic through `TOOL_SELF_REPORTED_PROBLEMS`; `skipped_groups` did not reach it.
- **Scientist view: `ResearchHarness._feed_result`** fed `json.dumps(result)[:4000]`. c135's run_de
  was 5,279 characters, so the Scientist got 221 of the 1,428 characters of its warnings. The
  DIRECTION BIAS, EXTREME FOLD-CHANGES and "6 groups NOT tested" warnings never reached it. Of the
  92 distinct archived results over 4000 characters (9 runs), 19 lost part of a reporting value this
  way. run_enrichment and run_gsea_prerank never fed their `errors` / `note` / `warnings` /
  `input_inference` at all, because those come after a 5–15K `top_terms_by_group`.

**The rule** (`agents/research_harness.py`): when a view has to drop something, it drops data, never
a reporting key.
- `_is_reporting_key` matches by name: `status`, `error(s)`, `warning(s)`, `note(s)`, `caveat(s)`,
  `limitations`, `inference`, `interpretation`, `skipped`, `skipped_groups`, and any `*_note(s)`,
  `*_warning(s)` or `*_inference`.
- `result_digest`: a dict keeps every reporting key plus its first 30 other keys, in the tool's
  order, and names what it leaves out (`"…": "+2 more keys: execution_mode, slurm_state"`). A dict
  with at most 30 other keys comes back unchanged. The order is kept on purpose, because
  `_digest_answer` (400 chars) and `claim_audit._evidence_for` (2,500 chars) read the digest's head,
  and that head is unchanged.
- `_feed_result`: a result of up to 4000 characters is fed verbatim. A longer one is fed with its
  reporting keys first and the rest in the tool's order, cut at the same 4000.

**Measured** on the 477 archived tool results:
- **Digest:** 4 results change; the total grows 1.03%. The three run_de digests grow by +1.6K (8847)
  and +2.4K (c135) characters, mostly the caveats they used to drop. One run_clustering digest
  gains a marker: its `cluster_sizes` has 33 clusters, and the Critic used to be shown 30 with no
  sign of the other 3.
- **Feed:** the total is unchanged (935,067 characters) and 25 results are reordered. Results
  losing part of a reporting value: 19 → 0.

**Trade-off in the feed.** The 4000 budget is fixed, so the caveats push out data from the end. For
c135's run_de, the Scientist no longer sees `significant_by_group`, `padj_threshold`,
`significance` or `n_significant_total` on that turn. The DIRECTION BIAS warning still lists the
up/down counts of four of the five strata. The Critic's digest keeps all of them, and
`step_numbers.py` reads full results. If the Scientist needs both, the next step is a superset feed:
the old 4000 characters plus the reporting text they cut. That would grow only the 19 affected
results, by 8 to 2,280 characters each.

**Still truncated, not changed:**
- The digest caps every string at 300 characters, which cuts most long warnings (7 of 10 run_de, 6
  of 6 run_pseudobulk_de). The Critic still reads them whole through `TOOL_SELF_REPORTED_PROBLEMS`,
  but the report writer gets the first 300 characters.
- `quick_chat._feed_result` has the same 4000 cut. Its tools are literature_search,
  map_phenotype_to_hpo and deep_literature.

**Tests:** `tests/test_tool_result_truncation.py` has 8 tests, built on c135's 38-key run_de result
key for key:
- the digest, and the payload the real `_critic` sends, keep `skipped_groups` and `warnings`;
- 8847's 36-key shape is digested whole;
- the harness loop feeds every warning within the same 4000 characters;
- a result that fits, and a result that is not a dict, are fed and digested exactly as before.

Five of the eight fail on the pre-fix code. The full suite passes (1906 passed, 3 skipped). The
comments on run_de's last key `cells_by_group_and_arm` (in `scrna_pack.py` and
`test_deg_contrast.py`) said it was last "so it never pushes `warnings` out". Position no longer
protects the warnings; the key stays last so that it is cut before the per-stratum counts.

**Status:** not deployed. Only the gateway changes (`research_harness.py`); the `scrna_pack.py`
change is a comment.

## 2026-09-28 — claim audit before writing; per-role effort with no wall-clock limit; execution defects

**Claim audit (`agents/claim_audit.py`).** Asked alone, both Qwens answered the checks their reports
failed (depth artefact, ambient RNA) correctly, 2/2 (3645cf3). While writing a long report they do
not apply them. So before either writer runs, the candidate headline findings are extracted and
each goes through the applicable checks, one short call per check: global shift under a depth gap,
gene/cell-type plausibility, pseudoreplicated p/FDR, causal wording. The design facts come from
the dataset profile. The verdicts bind `_synthesize` and `_build_report` and are listed in the
technical report's "Claim audit". Stage C (3.8 INT4, 3 reports per arm, 2 blind judges):
overclaims 83% -> 33%, invents 50% -> 0%, quality 6.0 -> 8.67, raises the depth artefact 67% -> 100%.
v2 (after the judges read v1) states the dataset's per-arm counts and depth ratio as authoritative
and drops the "ROBUST" label. Still left: FDR-filtered GSEA term counts reported as findings. A rule
against that was added but not re-measured. `BIOAGENT_CLAIM_AUDIT=0` turns it off.

**Effort and timeouts (Yijun: no timeout on planning, think longer).** Lab calls on the cluster GPU
stream with an IDLE timeout (`BIOAGENT_LAB_IDLE_TIMEOUT`, 300 s of silence) instead of a 600 s total.
Effort is per role: `BIOAGENT_VLLM_REASONING_EFFORT_{PLAN,WRITER,CRITIC,AUDIT,CLASSIFY}`. Prod runs
xhigh / xhigh / medium / medium / low, with global medium. **Qwen3.8 accepts only low / medium /
xhigh** and answers "high" with HTTP 400; a rejected value is retried with the model default. Real
PI prompt: xhigh plan 517 s (complete, and it caught the snRNA mito threshold); medium 133 s. The
gateway's manuscript writer had been called with no role.

**Execution audit (17 runs / 1,402 calls, subagent; each item re-verified).** Fixed in 2cdb91b:
- A returned `{status: error}` (25% of calls) reset the stuck-guard, which never fired.
- `inspect_dataset` let a model-typed HPC3 path override the bound dataset.
- The home directory was hard-coded as `/data/homezvol0/<user>` (it does not exist; now read from
  `$HOME`).
- The reference root `<shared>/reference` does not exist (now `<lab_storage>/software/reference`).
- Tool-less steps went to literature on the word "reference" (now judged by the headline).
- scGPT on mouse data (17/33,696 genes) is fixed separately in da52541 (automatic case-fold,
  tested live).

Still open: Qwen3.8 has had **no real execution run**. Stage B of the A/B and a headless run are the
next test.

## 2026-09-28 — a step's answer is checked against its own tool results

Run 8847d521ba32, stratified DE step (`run_de(groupby=sampleid, reference=WT,
stratify_by=majorclass)`). The tool got it right and the answer got it wrong:

| group | run_de / the dataset | the step's final answer |
|---|---|---|
| Endothelial (skipped) | DDX41 7 / WT 27 | DDX41 27 / WT 7 |
| Microglia (skipped) | DDX41 60 / WT 24 | DDX41 24 / WT 60 |
| AC (tested) | DDX41 392 / WT 258 | WT 469 / DDX41 181 |

All five unequal skipped pairs were swapped. The tested classes got a per-arm split that no tool
had computed: each class total was kept, but the split reverses the composition direction for AC,
BC and Cone. The Critic accepted the step at 0.95. Why nothing caught it:

- **The Scientist saw the right numbers.** `skipped_groups` sits at character 2693 of the 4000 it
  is fed, and it still read `n_condition` / `n_reference` the wrong way round.
- **The Critic never saw them.** `result_digest` keeps the first 30 keys of a tool result, and
  `skipped_groups` is key 33 of run_de's 36.
- **The writer was left to map roles to arms.** Its grounding facts said `condition=7,
  reference=27`. The archived report has three of the five pairs backwards (Endothelial,
  Microglia, Pericyte) and two right.

**The check** (`agents/step_numbers.py`, no model involved):
- **What it reads.** Per-(group, arm) cell counts in the answer, in two forms. The first is table
  rows under an arm-named count column ("WT cells", "n_condition"). The second is a group mention
  followed by per-arm counts: "Endothelial (WT: 7, DDX41: 27)", or "Endothelial (27 WT / 7 DDX41
  cells), HC (…)", where each group owns the text up to the next group or the end of its sentence.
  It ignores percentages, decimals, approximations, and anything framed as genes, depth, median or
  log-FC. A list introduced as genes is not read as cells.
- **What counts as truth.**
  - *This step's own tool results are exact:* `run_de.skipped_groups` (arms named from
    `condition`/`reference`, or parsed from `comparison` for old results) and
    `cells_by_group_and_arm`.
  - *The dataset profile (`design_by_arm.cells_by_label_and_arm`) is a CEILING only.* A count above
    the raw file's is impossible. A count below it is never flagged, because QC may have removed
    cells.
  - *The profile is dropped* when more than one dataset is bound, or when it scanned only part of
    the file.
- **What `_critic` does with it.**
  - `ANSWER_CONTRADICTS_TOOL_RESULTS` goes at the top of the payload.
  - A **second deterministic floor**: accept becomes revise, the score is capped at 0.5, and the
    critique carries the right values ("Endothelial: … run_de.skipped_groups computed DDX41=7,
    WT=27 — the two arms are swapped."). The retry is briefed with them.
  - The `numbers_contradicted` event becomes a ⚠ progress line.
  - Skipped for literature steps (other studies' cells) and for the deterministic digest answer.
  - A contradicted answer is never accepted, so it never reaches `_accepted_findings_block`, the
    team interpretation, or `_synthesize`.

**Tool changes** (they run on HPC3 from the synced source dir, so deploying needs the normal code
sync but no image rebuild):
- `run_de` (stratified) now returns `condition` by name, next to `reference`.
- `run_de` also returns `cells_by_group_and_arm` for every stratum, tested or skipped. It is the
  last key on purpose, so it never pushes `warnings` out of the Scientist's 4000 characters.
- `run_composition` returns `cells_by_group_and_arm`, the counts behind `pct_by_condition`.

**Report grounding:** `_uncovered_groups` now writes "(DDX41=7, WT=27)" instead of
"(condition=7, reference=27)" whenever the contrast says which arm is which.

**Measured before shipping** on every archived run_state.json (10 runs, 71 step answers):
- **Step answers.** The 8847 DE step is the only answer flagged: 22 claims bound, 10 groups wrong
  (5 swapped, 5 impossible). There were no false positives.
- **Final reports.** 8847's three swapped pairs are flagged. c135ae589d96's correct six-group
  enumeration binds 12 claims and passes.

**Tests:** `tests/test_step_numbers.py` has 27 tests. They replay 8847 through `_critic`, and
through a full `lab.run` whose model Critic accepts every attempt: round 1 is forced to revise,
round 2 is accepted with the corrected table, and the writer's prompt contains no swapped row. There
are also new tests for the run_de, run_composition, `_uncovered_groups` and progress-line changes.
The full suite passes with `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src`.

**Costs and limits:**
- **A flagged step costs one revise round.** The retry re-runs the step's tools, so for run_de that
  is one more Slurm job.
- **Only cell counts per (group, arm) are checked.** DEG counts, percentages and fold changes are
  not, and neither are claims whose arm the text does not name.
- **Older run_de results** have no `cells_by_group_and_arm`, so their tested groups are held only
  to the profile ceiling. That still catches 8847 (a wrong split with the right total must
  overshoot on one arm) but not an under-count.
- **Status:** not deployed.
- **Open, not fixed here:** the 30-key Critic digest and the Scientist's 4000-character feed both
  drop the tail of large tool results (run_de's `skipped_groups` and `warnings`).
- **Available, not wired in:** the same function flags the archived 8847 report's swaps. Wiring it
  into `verify_report_facts` as a Diagnostics audit would be a small follow-up.

## 2026-09-28 — post-mortem: two gpt-6-astra runs, 17.9 hours, no report

| | run 1 `ed4cfce52a2a` | run 2 `3c5fbc8608a7` |
|---|---|---|
| wall clock | **14.2 h** | **3.7 h** |
| rounds / accepted | 16 / 12 | 24 / 11 |
| `run_code` calls | 75 | 85 |
| `scgpt_annotate` calls | **0** | **0** |
| cost | unrecorded (no `llm_calls` yet) | **$7.53** |
| ended | stopped by hand | API key limit, mid-write-up |
| report the system produced | only because Stop ran the write-up | **none** |

Neither run failed at reasoning. Every step that produced nothing was scored correctly by the
Critic, wrote an honest account of what it could not do, and was right to refuse. The failures
were environmental, and each one was invisible.

**Run 1 — 92% of execution was a package nobody needed.** Every `run_code` call sat a flat
**10.1 minutes** between `tool_start` and the Slurm submit; 70 calls, **11.8 hours**. The
determinism preamble prepends a `try: import torch … except Exception: pass`, `ast.walk` descends
into try bodies, so torch counted as a hard dependency of every snippet, was probed in its own
container each time, and nothing cached the answer. The scGPT step was separately dead: its brief
said "reference", the literature classifier read the prose, and the guard that should have vetoed
that only recognised names beginning with `run_` — which nine of twenty-two tools do not.

**Run 2 — preflight fixed, and the blockers moved.** Gap per `run_code` call fell to **0.35 min
mean** (measured over all 85), saving roughly fourteen hours against run 1's rate. Per-round time
barely moved (11.8 → 10.9 min) because the work per round rose and three new blockers took over:
the provenance gate shipped that morning could only say no, so six of fifteen steps degraded to
detection rates with zero expression effects; the pathway step burned **nine rounds (~90 min)**
blocked twice over — no effects to analyse, and a gene-set collection it believed it had to
download while three verified `.gmt` libraries sat in the directory the enrichment tools read
from; and scGPT was blocked *again*, by a model directory missing from `read_roots` and by
`read_tool_source` returning the dispatcher instead of the injected runner.

**Four properties this shares with every other failure found this month.**

1. *Environmental, not cognitive.* A false dependency. A regex over prose. A path missing from a
   list. A dispatcher hiding its implementation. Files on disk nobody mentioned. A token ceiling
   measured against one model. A rule written with no exit.
2. *Invisible.* 1,700+ unit tests green throughout. Error channels clean. The Critic scored the
   scGPT refusal **0.95** — correctly, because refusing was right on what the step could see.
3. *Discipline made it worse.* A sloppier agent would have normalised the un-provenanced matrix
   and produced fold changes. This one refused, correctly by the rule it was given, and produced
   nothing. Rigour with no escape hatch is indistinguishable from paralysis.
4. *Retry cannot clear an environmental block.* The failure fork proposed alternatives twice per
   node and the pathway step retried nine times. Every alternative was a different METHOD; the
   obstacles were a missing read path and a missing input. None of the nine could have worked.

**Fixed since (all live):** preflight memoisation + the guarded-import rule; tool-name routing;
the two-sided provenance gate; `read_roots` covering the model dirs; `dispatches_to` on
`read_tool_source`; the skills naming the local `.gmt` files; `describe_environment` and
`docs/ENVIRONMENT.md`; the Scientist closing turn and retry escalation; the run-level HITL cap of
three; `finish_reason` + retry-once on a truncated reply; `llm_calls` cost accounting; and runs
left `running` by a restart now close as `interrupted` (twelve had accumulated since July, so every
success rate computed from that table was wrong).

**What the post-mortem cost in wrong diagnoses, recorded because the pattern repeats.** Three
causes were asserted and disproven by measurement (waiting on HPC3; waiting on the model; the
artifact mirror). A "7.4× faster" claim came from a probe field that was not run start. A
follow-up plan was blamed on a `report.md` placed by hand, when the real cause was a plan reply
truncated at the token ceiling — found only because `llm_calls` had been built that same morning.
**The table that catches a defect usually has to exist before the defect is legible.**

## 2026-09-26 — switching the cluster model keeps the card when it fits

The serve job is now a small **supervisor** (`gpu._serve_script`). It serves whatever
`~/.bioagent/vllm.<jobid>.spec` names (line 1 is the model tag, line 2 the base64 serve body). When
the gateway rewrites the spec, the supervisor stops vLLM as a whole session (setsid + kill session
and group), waits for the port to be free, and starts the new model on the same card and the same
port. A vLLM crash still ends the job, as before.

`gpu.ensure_serve_job`: a running job that serves another model is **switched in place** when
`card_fits`, i.e. the model has no `cards` restriction or the card nvidia-smi reports is one of its
allowed gres types. Otherwise, or for a pre-supervisor job, it is released and a new one is queued.
The Slurm comment is updated with `scontrol update ... Comment=` (allowed for your own job).
Cluster models gained `cards` (e.g. `RTX6000,A100`), which also filters the race candidates for a
new job. The gateway keeps the existing tunnel (same job/node/port) and waits until `/v1/models`
names the NEW model, because the old one answers on that port for a few seconds.

Verified on HPC3 (job 56973657, RTX PRO 6000): 3.8 → 3.6 in 157 s and 3.6 → 3.8 in 161 s, on the
same job, port and tunnel. Only one vLLM + EngineCore was left after each switch, and tool calls
were correct. The supervisor noticed the NFS spec change in about 9 s.

## 2026-09-25 (later) — "Cluster GPU" is now a list of models, with a 🖥 manager

HPC3 now holds several weight sets. "Cluster GPU" used to be a single entry, and the only way to
change the model was an env var plus a restart. New module `gateway/cluster_models.py`: each entry
is repo + vLLM image + quantization + args, because a repo alone gives 3.8 weights to a vLLM that
cannot read them. The list is lab-wide and admin-edited, stored in
`$BIOAGENT_STATE_DIR/cluster_models.json`, and seeded from the env plus `KNOWN` (3.8 INT4, 3.6 AWQ).

- **Picker:** `Cluster GPU · <label>` per enabled model (default marked), in both the login and
  session pickers. `/api/connect` takes `cluster_model_id`. `/api/llm-endpoint` with a different
  cluster model starts a new serve job (`_reprovision_gpu`: releases the old card first). It is
  refused (409) while a run is in progress.
- **🖥 dialog:** every entry, whether its weights are in the HF cache and how big they are (one
  `ls`+`du` over the session's SSH), and HF-cache weights nobody registered yet. Admins can make
  default, enable/disable, edit, add and remove. Remove only takes the row off the list; the weights
  stay on HPC3.
- **Limit:** serve jobs are single-GPU, so DSV4 / GLM-5.3 (4-card) are listed as on-disk but cannot
  run from here.

## 2026-09-25 — the served model is now Qwen3.8-27B (INT4), and the free RTX6000 is tried first

There is no official Qwen3.8 35B-A3B. The runnable 3.8 is **Qwen3.8-27B**: dense, hybrid
attention, with a vision tower. Measured on HPC3, with one card, 262144 ctx, and the same bench
(`/dfs3b/ruic20_lab/software/AiScientist/qwen38_test/`):

| model | card | 1-stream tok/s | 8-way tok/s | KV tokens | tool calls | ready |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ | A100 | 146 | 898 | 2.47M | 5/5 | 280s |
| Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ | RTX6000 | 175 | 982 | 3.03M | 5/5 | 190s |
| **Qwen3.8-27B-INT4** (RedHatAI) | A100 | 70 | 459 | 1.63M | 5/5 | 410s |
| **Qwen3.8-27B-INT4** (RedHatAI) | RTX6000 | 75 | 516 | 2.02M | 5/5 | 390s |
| Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4 (nvidia) | A100 | 62 | 403 | 0.79M | 5/5 | 530s |
| Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4 (nvidia) | RTX6000 | 63 | 354 | 0.97M | 5/5 | 640s |

- **NVFP4 does run on A100.** vLLM 0.28 falls back to `MarlinNvFp4LinearKernel` (weight-only).
  Even native Blackwell is not faster, and it halves the KV pool. INT4 wins on every axis.
- **A 27B dense model costs about 2.3x the decode time per token** of the 3B-active MoE. That is the
  price of the quality gain. Plan and write-up quality have NOT been re-measured on the A/B
  harness yet.
- **Defaults moved** to `RedHatAI/Qwen3.8-27B-INT4`, `vllm-0.28.0.sif`, and quantization
  auto-detect. The 3.6 weights and `vllm.sif` are kept. Rollback is three env vars; see the
  comment in `gateway/settings.py`.
- **`BIOAGENT_GPU_PREFER_SECONDS`** makes the GPU race ordered. A later-listed card that starts
  first is held until the earlier ones have queued that long. Prod now lists the free RTX6000
  first and the paid A100 second. Caveat: `free-gpu32` is PreemptMode=CANCEL, so preemption kills
  the session's server.
- **Serve jobs are tagged `--comment=model=<id>`.** A running job for a different (or untagged)
  model is cancelled rather than reused. Without the tag, the first reconnect after a model switch
  died at `ensure_model`.
- **The vLLM load wait went from 10 to 20 min.** The 3.8 cold start (530–640s for NVFP4) exceeded
  the old window.

## 2026-09-14 (night, addendum 2) — the step's turn budget was invisible to the model spending it

The Scientist gets 8 tool turns per step. Nothing ever told it so. Round 16 of run ed4cfce52a2a
splits cleanly on that:

| stop_reason | rounds | mean Critic score | outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| `finished` | 9 | **0.73** | 9 of 9 accepted |
| `max_steps` | 5 | **0.52** | 3 of 5 revise |

Exhausting the budget roughly halves the odds of acceptance, and the mechanism is not that those
steps were worse. A step that cannot see the cap spends turn 8 starting more analysis, is cut off
mid-flight, and hands the Critic a deterministic auto-summary of its tool returns instead of its
own account of the results. The Critic is judging the wrong artifact.

Two changes, neither of which raises the cap — on the Slurm path every extra turn is another job.

**The model is told.** With `warn_turns_left` (2) remaining, one injected line says how many turns
are left and to stop starting new analysis. Injected once, not per turn: this loop already trims
its own history to fit the window, and repeating the notice would push out the work.

**A step that runs out anyway gets one closing turn.** Text-only, and any tool call made in it is
discarded — it is a way to spend the last word, not to reopen the budget. It buys what the cap
denied (a real answer over results already on disk) for one cheap call rather than another Slurm
round. `stop_reason` becomes `closing_turn`; the deterministic digest stays the fallback, so a
failed closing turn costs one call and changes nothing else.

And the retry no longer walks into the same wall: `_budget_bonus` gives a revision that follows a
`max_steps` attempt four extra turns, eight on a second. Only that stop reason earns them — a
revise for wrong CONTENT is not a request for more room, and more turns would buy a longer version
of the same mistake. Run ed4cfce52a2a spent rounds 14 AND 15 on one step, both `max_steps`, both
revise, because the retry re-ran under the cap the first attempt had already disproved.

Eight tests. The two that matter most are the negative ones: a normally-finishing step spends no
extra call, and a closing turn that errors still ships the step.

## 2026-09-14 (night, addendum) — Stop ended the analysis and then spent eleven more minutes

The same run was stopped by hand at step 18. The analysis loop halted at once — and the gateway
then spent **15:50:35 → 16:01:46** on the write-up: the report (a model call), the self-review
(another), the PDF and DOCX renders, the post-render text review, the post-render visual review on
HPC3, and the technical report. Five model calls and two renders, on a metered key, after a button
whose only message was "Stop requested — ending the current run."

None of that is a broken Stop. `should_cancel` is wired to the lab loop and nothing below it, and
`_run_produced_nothing` says so on purpose: *"A mid-run Stop that accepted ≥1 step still writes up
what ran."* That salvage is worth keeping — this run's abandoned 12/20 steps became a 4,000-word
manuscript that states its own gaps (no scGPT labels, no replication, a depth confound it refuses
to interpret past). Losing it to a click would have been the worse failure. What was missing was a
way to decline it, and any hint that it was happening.

So Stop now has two levels. The first says what it is doing — *"Analysis stopped — writing up the
N step(s) that completed. This takes a few minutes. Press Stop again to skip the write-up."* The
second sets `RunState.hard_stop`, and the tail skips every model call and both renders, still
writing `report/report.md` (deterministic, naming the steps that completed) and the run state, so
the bundle and its resume path survive. Abandoning costs prose, never data — every figure and table
it would have described was already on disk when Stop was pressed. `hard_stop` is cleared where
`chat_stop` is, because `_run_lab` reuses `conn.active_run` and a leaked flag would make the NEXT
run skip a report it was never asked to skip.

Four tests in `tests/test_run_isolation.py`, including one that pins the single-Stop salvage —
without it, the escalation test would pass just as well if the write-up had simply broken.

## 2026-09-14 (night) — twelve hours of one real run found four structural defects that 1,716 unit tests did not

A single production run (`ed4cfce52a2a`, a DDX41-vs-WT retina `.h5ad` routed to a user's own
gpt-6-astra key) was left to execute a 20-step plan while being watched. It took twelve hours to
reach step 13. Everything below was found by measuring that run, not by reading code — and every
one of them was invisible to a green test suite. Four commits on
`claude/gpt6-astra-cost-monitoring-c79415`, **none deployed** (a deploy restarts the gateway and
kills whatever run is live).

**A step named a tool and the tool was never called.** Plan step 1 read ``Run `scgpt_annotate` …
to request reference-transferred labels``. `scgpt_annotate` was called zero times. The step was
classified as a LITERATURE step, ran four `literature_search` calls, ended on
`literature_search_multi_query`, scored **revise 0.1** — and because literature steps force-advance
instead of retrying, the whole scGPT arm died while the run reported progress. `_is_literature_step`
reads the step's PROSE, where `reference` is a literature signal; the guard that should have vetoed
it matched only names beginning with `run_`, and nine of the catalog's twenty-two tools do not —
`scgpt_annotate`, `annotate_variants`, `map_phenotype_to_hpo`, `diagnose_disease`,
`inspect_dataset` among them. "Reference genome", "reference allele" and "reference atlas" are not
optional vocabulary here, so the same trap was armed for the variant and phenotype pipelines. A
step's role now comes from the TOOL it declares (intersected with the real catalog, because
backticks also carry obs column names); prose is the fallback only when no tool is named. The
literature keep-set named `run_literature`/`run_paperqa`, neither of which the catalog serves —
corrected in the same change, or the fix would have misrouted genuine literature steps the other
way. Note this is the SECOND time this classifier has cost a plan its central step; the code
comment records `run_de(reference="WT")` doing it before.

**Ninety-two percent of execution was one wasted check.** Every `run_code` call sat for a flat
**10.1 minutes** between `tool_start` and the Slurm submit — 70 calls, 11.8 hours, never varying.
The job itself averaged 18 seconds. Cause: the determinism preamble prepends
`try: import torch as _bio_t; _bio_t.manual_seed(0) except Exception: pass`, `third_party_imports`
walks the assembled code with `ast.walk` (which descends into try bodies) and counted that as a
hard dependency, and `missing_modules` probes each module in its own container on the worker node
with nothing caching the answer. An import the snippet itself gives up on is not a dependency —
but the distinction is the HANDLER, not the try: `except Exception: pass` has nothing to do on
failure, while `except ImportError: cyvcf2 = None` binds a fallback and means to use the module
anyway. An existing test pinned the cyvcf2 case and broke a first pass that treated every guarded
import as optional; that test was right. Probe results are now memoized per session (status-0 only:
an image cannot lose a module mid-run, while a probe that merely failed to execute says nothing).

**A run's spend existed nowhere, and the metered path had no ceiling.** `complete()` read the
response body for its `content` and discarded `usage`, so the only instrument was the monthly bill,
which cannot say WHICH call was expensive. Worse, remote completions carried no `max_tokens` at
all: the lab computes a budget but the injected `(messages) -> str` contract had nowhere to put it,
so a number meant as context-fitting for the cluster's own window became no limit whatsoever on a
per-token API. Lab roles now carry a role, a metered endpoint gets a per-role ceiling
(`BIOAGENT_LAB_MAX_TOKENS` / `_WRITER`), and every call writes an `llm_calls` row — tokens, the
provider-reported USD, and the ceiling actually sent. The cluster GPU path is byte-for-byte
unchanged. The local path also gained `BIOAGENT_VLLM_REASONING_EFFORT`: it could switch thinking on
and off but not say how hard.

**141 tables reached disk and none of them was evidence.** Every Critic verdict on a `run_code`
step said a version of "none of the claimed output CSVs appears in the tool evidence; each call
lists only the input H5AD". `run_code`'s result carried stdout and a return code and nothing else,
so `evidence_pointers` found no artifact — which pins every such step in the 0.6-0.8 band ("a
material claim rests on prose with no backing artifact") and, because the report writer may cite
only what an accepted step produced, leaves the manuscript unable to reference tables that existed
the whole time. Fifteen of the twenty planned steps used `run_code`, so three quarters of the plan
could produce no citable evidence by construction. The scores read as a slide (0.76, 0.72, 0.68,
0.65) but were a ceiling. `SlurmCodeExecutor` now snapshots the remote artifacts directory before
and after the job and reports the difference under `tables`/`figures`/`artifacts` — the keys
`evidence_pointers` already collects. The snapshot carries mtimes, not just paths, because a
revision REWRITES a table and a path-set diff would call that no output at all.

**And one thing that was NOT the problem.** All 141 local tables shared one mtime, which looked
like the artifact mirror re-fetching everything on every call — it does, 168 files over SFTP per
`run_code`. Decomposing all 70 calls showed the window containing collect + mirror + the model's
next turn averaged 1.0 min early and 0.6 min late: it never grew, and the interval growth that
prompted the look was the Slurm jobs themselves getting longer (4-15s early, 64-187s late) as the
analyses got bigger. The mirror is now incremental anyway — it scales with file count AND file
size, so one step writing a large checkpoint would make it genuinely expensive — but it is a
scaling fix, not a speedup, and the commit says so.

**Skills and pipelines.** `scgpt_annotation` was the only pipeline in the library with neither an
enrichment nor a literature tool, and its closing line — "The report is assembled automatically —
do NOT plan a report-writing step" — reads to a PI as "interpretation is downstream, plan nothing".
Combined with the report writer's anti-fabrication rule (it may name only pathways and citations
that appear in an accepted step), that made the biology unreachable by construction rather than by
oversight. Both `scgpt_annotation` and `celltype_annotation` now require the interpretation
EVIDENCE — `run_enrichment`, `literature_search` — and say what happens without it, and the
closing line distinguishes writing the report from interpreting the results. Pipeline selection
also learned to check whether a pipeline's tools can actually run here (`HarnessTool.enabled`), so
a run can no longer announce "Loaded preset pipeline: scGPT foundation-model annotation" for a tool
that cannot execute.

**Two smaller surfaces.** `quality 0.76` now renders with its rubric band — the bands were data
only inside a prompt string, and a test fails if they drift from what the Critic is told. And the
design meeting's literature (eleven real DOI-backed lookups in this run) gets its own
`## Background consulted during study design` section: `## References` is built only from
Critic-accepted analysis steps by design, and merging meeting reading into it would claim support
that was never established, while discarding it — the previous behaviour — hides work that shaped
the plan.

**What this says about the tests.** The suite was green through all four defects and stayed green
at 1,716 passing after the fixes; none of these is the kind of thing a unit test sees, because each
is a property of a long run against a real cluster. What the tests DID catch, twice, was a bad
design of mine: the cyvcf2 fallback above, and a second where reading `cache._present` directly
made a cache-shaped stub raise an AttributeError that `PreflightingExecutor` swallows as "preflight
skipped" — which in production, where the real cache has the field, would have had no symptom at
all while silently disabling dependency resolution on some paths.

**Left undone, deliberately.** The local `CodeSandbox` still declares no artifacts (production runs
`run_code` on Slurm; the local path has a different shape and is better done deliberately than by
half). `_TOOL_CALL_RE`, which plan-time validation uses, has the same `run_`-only blind spot as the
routing guard did — so "the plan names a tool that does not exist" is unverifiable for those same
nine tools. And the Scientist's tool-turn budget is tight for the heavy descriptive steps: two
rounds ended `incomplete max_steps`, and step 18 spent 2.5 hours across two attempts and 13
`run_code` calls.

## 2026-09-14 (later) — the model field now answers "what do I put here?", and the store move had a tail

**The model box is a search.** Feedback from the dialog: a plain text box asks the user to know a
model id by heart, and OpenRouter alone serves ~450. It is now a combobox over the endpoint's OWN
ids — click to open, type to filter (with the match highlighted), click or Enter to fill, ↑/↓ to
walk, Escape to dismiss. It stays a text input underneath, because endpoints that publish no
catalogue still need one and a picked id must stay editable. Enter with nothing highlighted takes
the top match rather than saving a half-typed id — unless what you typed IS a real id, or there is
nothing to complete with, in which case it submits as before.

The list no longer waits for ↻ either: it is fetched as soon as the form holds enough to fetch it
with — a saved endpoint being edited (key already on file) or a base URL and key both filled in —
and silently, so a half-finished form never throws errors at someone who has not asked for
anything. ↻ remains, as "re-read it". Two layout fixes came out of driving it: the model inputs
were at their default ~150px while every other field ran the full width (the field holding the
longest values was the smallest box), and clicking a field that already had focus fires no focus
event, so the menu stayed shut and read as broken.

**The store move needed a second pass.** Moving the credential stores to `/data/BioAgent/state`
left every row pointing at a file that was no longer there: rows carry the `key_path` they were
written with, and rows written while `BIOAGENT_STATE_DIR` was unset hold a path RELATIVE to the
then-current working directory (`llm_creds/<owner>/<id>.key`). The keys were intact on disk and
every use of them failed — "This credential's key file is outside the owner's store" for an LLM
key, and for SSH the stale path goes straight to paramiko, so saved-key login silently drops back
to password + Duo. Caught by probing the live endpoint; the earlier verification had only checked
LISTINGS, which are metadata and never touch the key.

Fixed in two places. The data: every row re-pointed at the file that is actually there (backup
first, `/data/BioAgent/creds-backup-20260914-prerepath.tgz`), then confirmed by asking OpenRouter
for its catalogue with the stored key — 445 models. The code: both stores now derive the key
location from the CURRENT store and treat the recorded `key_path` as a fallback for older layouts,
so a move is self-healing. That changed what tampering with `index.json` does — the recorded path
is simply not consulted while the real key is in its canonical place, so the old
"refuses-with-an-error" test became "ignores it and returns the right key", with the refusal still
pinned for the one case that does consult it.

## 2026-09-14 — the users' keys were living inside the rsync target (prod ops change, DONE)

Checking the new credential dialog against the live box: `BIOAGENT_STATE_DIR` was unset in the prod
`.env`, so `ssh_credentials` and `llm_credentials` resolved their stores against the systemd
`WorkingDirectory` and sat at `/data/BioAgent/app/{ssh_creds,llm_creds}` — inside the directory
`scripts/sync_deploy.sh` rsyncs into, and absent from its exclude list, the list that exists so
`.env`, the DB and `runs/` survive a deploy. `sync_deploy.sh --delete` — the mode the script's own
help recommends when the server tree is divergent — would have deleted 5 users' HPC3 private keys
(BioAdmin, jinl14, teacher, ziyaom2, Ziyaoma) and the saved API keys. No deploy had used
`--delete`, so nothing was lost.

Done, in this order, with the console restarted once at the end:

1. `tar` of both stores -> `/data/BioAgent/creds-backup-20260914.tgz` (0600), contents listed and
   checked (7 `.key` files + indexes); `.env` copied to `.env.bak-20260914`.
2. `cp -a` both stores into a new `/data/BioAgent/state` (**0700**, `aiscientist`), `diff -r`
   clean against the originals and the 0600 modes preserved.
3. `BIOAGENT_STATE_DIR=/data/BioAgent/state` appended to the prod `.env` — comment lines ABOVE the
   value, never inline, because systemd's `EnvironmentFile` keeps an inline `#` as part of the value.
4. `scripts/sync_deploy.sh` (main@5447f88), which restarts through systemd and so re-reads the file.
5. Verified from the server: `storage.dir` now reports `/data/BioAgent/state/llm_creds/BioAdmin`,
   all five SSH keys and both LLM endpoints still list. Then the app-dir copies were REMOVED and
   every listing re-checked — still there, which is what proves the app reads the new path.
   The app dir stays clean under API traffic.

Safer than before in a second way: the stores were in a world-readable app dir with 0664
`index.json`; `/data/BioAgent/state` is 0700, so no other account on the box can even list it.
`--delete` is safe again — and both paths are excluded from the deploy anyway (5447f88).

`BIOAGENT_DATABASE_URL` is set to Postgres in prod, which is the only reason moving the state dir
did not also move the accounts DB: `db.py` falls back to `<state>/bioagent.db` when it is unset.
`/data/BioAgent/app/bioagent.db` is a dead sqlite relic from 2026-06-18.

**Noticed in passing:** BioAdmin's two saved endpoints are both OpenRouter with the same key and an
EMPTY model — the exact residue of the bug fixed in 6c9bff5 (save first, then discover you cannot
list models, then be refused at bind time). They can now be repaired in the dialog: ✎ -> ↻ -> pick
a model -> Save changes, or 🗑 one of the duplicates.

## 2026-09-12 — the BYO-API-key dialog had a cycle in it (branch `claude/external-api-integration-issue-967d46`, NOT merged)

Reported from the UI: "you have to save before you can list, and after saving the option can't be
added to or changed, and it isn't clear where the key is kept — the provider only shows it once."
All four are the same shape of problem, and the first one is a genuine cycle:

* `/api/llm-endpoint` refuses a credential with no model id ("Set a model on this credential"),
* the model ids live on the provider, and
* the only route that could read them, `GET /api/llm-credentials/{id}/models`, needed a SAVED
  credential.

So ↻ answered "Save this endpoint first" and the first save always produced an endpoint that
appeared in the picker and could not be used.

**Backend.** New `llm_providers.probe_models()` — the same `GET /models` call as `list_models()`
but keeping the REASON (auth / credit / endpoint / network / unsupported), because an empty list
and a wrong key look identical otherwise. New `POST /api/llm-models` lists models for an endpoint
that is still being typed. It accepts `credential_id` to reuse a stored key, and then uses that
credential's OWN base URL — a stored key aimed at a URL from the request would be a way to read the
key back out of the server. `PUT /api/llm-credentials/{id}` now re-proves a metadata edit with the
key on file (a model change drops the verification tick, and re-testing it is what turns "did the
id I picked work?" from a second click into part of the save), returns the result as `verify`, and
accepts `provider`, which it silently ignored before. `GET /api/llm-credentials` now carries
`storage` (owner, directory, 0600, encrypted-at-rest flag).

**Frontend.** ↻ works with nothing saved; the ids land in a visible picker under the field, not
only in a `datalist` nobody finds. Saving without a model id is refused in the dialog instead of at
bind time. The endpoint picker (both the login form and the connected panel) grew ✎ and 🗑 that act
on the selected endpoint — deleting the one a live session is using switches that session back to
the cluster GPU first. The dialog states where the key is stored and that an edit never needs the
key again. Owner resolution now goes through one helper (`apiKeyOwner`), which also reads the
UCInetID back from the connection summary — with accounts off, after a reload the login input is
empty and the dialog was looking in the anonymous `guest` store.

1702 tests pass (16 new). Verified live against a local gateway + a stub OpenAI-compatible endpoint:
list-before-save, pick, save, auto-select, edit via ✎, rotate-free edit, delete-while-active.

**Pre-existing and NOT fixed here:** with accounts ON, the credential routes still honour a `user=`
query parameter from a caller with no session, so `GET /api/llm-credentials?user=someone` lists that
user's endpoint metadata (masked keys, never the secret). That is the whole `_cred_owner` family —
SSH credentials included — and tightening it breaks the accounts-off path the tests exercise, so it
needs its own change.

## 2026-09-02 — the hypothesis ledger could only confirm (branch `feat/discriminating-hypotheses`, NOT merged)

Run `c135ae589d96` (Ddx41 mouse retina) ran with `BIOAGENT_HYPOTHESIS_DRIVEN=1` and generated
exactly one hypothesis — "the shifts are driven by a 1.6x depth imbalance" — and marked it
`supported`. It had no rival, so every result merely consistent with it read as support; and its
prediction ("pseudobulk will collapse the log2FCs") is guaranteed by one library per arm, so the
outcome was fixed by the design rather than by which explanation was true. The run concluded the
opposite of the published result for that model (Mars et al. 2026, medRxiv
`10.64898/2026.01.28.26344834`: the signal is in Muller cells, in morphogenesis and junction
formation) — which its own tables carried.

`agents/hypotheses.py` was a good store with all its guards pointed at PLAN HYGIENE (orphan steps,
duplicates, busywork, caps) and none at the epistemic quality of the hypothesis. Three changes:

1. **A hypothesis carries its rival.** New fields `rival` and `discriminator`; `render()` prints
   the pair as a contest so the adjudicator at step 7 sees both sides of what it is deciding.
2. **`admit()` gates before `add()`**, returning a REASON that is emitted (`hypothesis_refused`)
   rather than dropping silently. Refuses: no rival; a rival that normalizes to the statement; no
   discriminator, or one that normalizes to the prediction; and — the h1 case — a discriminator
   resting on a between-arm significance test when `DesignFacts.replicates_per_arm < 2`, because
   both explanations predict "nothing significant" there.
3. **Adjudication is a contest.** `adjudicate(id, favoured)` with `favoured` in
   hypothesis/rival/neither maps to supported/refuted/inconclusive, so "supported" is only
   reachable by the evidence pushing AWAY from the rival. `resolve()` stays for replayed state.

`ResearchLab._design_facts()` derives the replication structure conservatively from the dataset
profile: if the richest non-annotation, non-QC obs column has at most two levels, nothing separates
samples within an arm (one replicate per arm); anything ambiguous returns unknown, and an unknown
design never blocks a hypothesis. Fed the REAL `obs_categoricals` from that run
(`orig.ident` 1, `sampleid` 2, `DF.classifications` 1) it derives 1 per arm and refuses the actual
h1 verbatim.

40 tests pass (11 new, including the h1 text pinned as a regression). Two pre-existing failures on
Python 3.9 (`sys.stdlib_module_names`) are unrelated — reproduced on pristine main.

**Not in this branch, and it is the other half:** the Critic scores fidelity to artifacts, never
validity of the inference — step 6 of that run scored **1.0** for faithfully restating a wrong
verdict. This branch makes the run generate a rival; a second line has to make the review notice
when the rival was right.

## 2026-08-20 (late) — the eight defects in the live all-Qwen plan, fixed at the framework level (main 764f214, deployed)

Yijun reviewed the plan the production PI wrote in the real console and found eight problems.
Each is fixed where it can recur.

**A new tool, `run_depth_matched_de`** (findings 1 and 6). The plan told the executor to
"correlate per-gene logFC against the between-arm median nCount_RNA" — not computable, a per-gene
vector against one scalar. Three models wrote three wrong versions across two runs and it failed
every time, leaving the report's central question (artefact or biology?) unanswered. The tool
quantile-matches the deeper arm onto the shallower arm's per-cell UMI distribution within each
cell type (capped at each cell's own counts), re-runs the SAME Wilcoxon contrast, and Spearman-
correlates the original ranking against the matched one PER DIRECTION.

**Validated against a known answer** (`experiments/depth_matched_validation/`), because no unit
test can show a method is scientifically right. Two synthetic cell types: one with a 2x depth
imbalance AND real biology, one with the same imbalance and NOTHING different. All four verdicts
correct — DepthOnly up/down both INVERTED (rho -0.16 / -0.05), RealBio up PRESERVED (rho 0.82),
RealBio down (no real biology there) correctly weak. Gene level: 30/30 of the genuinely changed
genes kept their rank, 3/30 of the background did. The first run of this validation FAILED its own
control and changed the tool: "depth-robust" had meant "kept its sign", which passes a gene whose
effect collapses from 3.0 to 0.02; it now means the gene stays in the matched ranking's own top-N.

**A deterministic guard in `run_enrichment`** (finding 3). The plan declared its p-values
non-inferential in the DE step, then passed padj=0.05 to enrichment — where that parameter is the
cutoff a gene must clear to ENTER the test, laundering a pseudoreplicated p-value into
